 C. Safety is a shared responsibility between employers, leads, and workers  
 D. Safety only matters when someone gets hurt

**Correct Answer:** C

## **7. Which of the following is a general worker safety right?**

A. The right to ignore PPE rules  
 B. The right to a safe workplace and safety information workers can understand  
 C. The right to operate any equipment on-site  
 D. The right to move gear without direction

**Correct Answer:** B

## **8. Which of the following is a worker responsibility?**

A. Follow safety rules and report hazards  
 B. Create your own work plan without asking  
 C. Enter restricted areas to look busy  
 D. Move department gear without permission

**Correct Answer:** A

## **9. What should a new stagehand do if they see a road case blocking an emergency exit?**

A. Ignore it unless someone complains  
 B. Sit on the case so people notice it  
 C. Report it to the correct lead immediately  
 D. Push it anywhere without asking

**Correct Answer:** C

## **10. What is a hazard?**

A. Something that could cause harm  
 B. A person who asks too many questions  
 C. A task that takes too long  
 D. A rule that only applies to supervisors

**Correct Answer:** A

## **11. What is risk?**

A. The chance that a hazard could cause harm and how serious the outcome could be  
 B. Any piece of gear with wheels  
 C. A worker who is new  
 D. The same thing as PPE

**Correct Answer:** A

## **12. What is unsafe behavior?**

A. Asking a lead for clarification  
 B. An action that increases danger  
 C. Waiting for a forklift path to clear  
 D. Reporting a wet floor

**Correct Answer:** B

## **13. Which example shows the difference between a hazard and a risk?**

A. A cable is the hazard; someone tripping over it is the risk  
 B. A lead is the hazard; a stagehand is the risk  
 C. A hard hat is the hazard; PPE is the risk  
 D. A break is the hazard; lunch is the risk

**Correct Answer:** A

## **14. Which condition can increase the risk of a cable path?**

A. Good lighting and clear markings  
 B. Low light, tired workers, and cases moving through the area  
 C. A clear walking path  
 D. A lead standing nearby

**Correct Answer:** B

## **15. What should a new stagehand do if they notice an unstable stack of road cases?**

A. Climb on the cases to test them  
 B. Push the stack to see if it moves  
 C. Stay clear and report it to the correct lead  
 D. Ignore it unless it falls

**Correct Answer:** C

## **16. What does a “pause and get a lead” mindset mean?**

A. Refuse every task  
 B. Stop the entire show whenever you are nervous  
 C. Pause your own action when something is unclear, unsafe, or outside your training  
 D. Leave the work area without telling anyone

**Correct Answer:** C

## **17. Which situation should cause a new stagehand to pause and ask for direction?**

A. They clearly understand the task and the path is safe  
 B. They are asked to pull a cable but do not know whether it is still connected  
 C. Their lead tells them where to stand  
 D. They are waiting for the next instruction

**Correct Answer:** B

## **18. What should a new stagehand say if asked to operate a lift they have not been trained or authorized to use?**

A. “I’ll try it carefully.”  
 B. “I have not been trained or authorized on that equipment.”  
 C. “I watched someone do it once.”  
 D. “I can probably figure it out.”

**Correct Answer:** B

## **19. What is a near miss?**

A. A task that was completed early  
 B. An event where someone could have been hurt, but was not  
 C. A stagehand who arrives late  
 D. A piece of gear that belongs to another department

**Correct Answer:** B

## **20. Why should near misses be reported?**

A. To blame someone  
 B. To slow down the crew  
 C. To help identify and correct hazards before someone gets hurt  
 D. To avoid telling the lead what happened

**Correct Answer:** C

## **21. What should happen first if someone is seriously hurt or there is immediate danger?**

A. Fill out a report before doing anything else  
 B. Follow the site emergency procedure and get help  
 C. Keep working until break  
 D. Post about it online

**Correct Answer:** B

## **22. Which of the following should be reported?**

A. An injury  
 B. Gear damage  
 C. A near miss  
 D. All of the above

**Correct Answer:** D

## **23. Why should a new stagehand never pretend to know something?**

A. Because pretending can lead to unsafe actions, damaged gear, and confusion  
 B. Because leads do not like questions  
 C. Because beginners are not allowed to learn  
 D. Because all tasks are too dangerous

**Correct Answer:** A

## **24. What should a new stagehand do during a safety briefing?**

A. Check their phone  
 B. Talk to another crew member  
 C. Listen for hazards, PPE rules, restricted areas, emergency information, and who their lead is  
 D. Walk away if they have worked at the venue before

**Correct Answer:** C

## **25. What does a safety briefing not do?**

A. Provide useful jobsite information  
 B. Explain hazards and procedures  
 C. Tell workers who to report to  
 D. Authorize a worker to perform specialized work they have not been trained and assigned to do

**Correct Answer:** D

# **Scenario Questions**

These can be used as written-response questions, discussion prompts, or instructor-reviewed answers.

## **Scenario 1: The Rushed Case Push**

You are working load-out. The crew is moving quickly, and someone tells you to push a road case across a dark area near the dock. You cannot clearly see the path, and you know forklifts have been active nearby.

**Question:** What should you do?

**Best Answer:**  
 Pause your own work and do not push blindly into the dark path. Move to a safe position if needed, communicate the concern, and ask the correct lead whether the path is clear or whether another route should be used. Speed should never replace safety.

## **Scenario 2: The Unknown Cable**

A crew member says, “Grab that cable and pull it back.” You are not sure what department it belongs to or whether it is still connected.

**Question:** What should you do?

**Best Answer:**  
 Do not pull the cable until it has been cleared by the correct department or lead. A good response would be, “Has this cable been cleared to pull?” or “Should we check with the department before moving it?”

## **Scenario 3: The Near Miss**

A road case starts rolling down a slight ramp and almost hits a worker, but someone stops it in time. Nobody is hurt.

**Question:** Should this be reported? Why or why not?

**Best Answer:**  
 Yes. This is a near miss. Even though nobody was hurt, the situation showed that someone could have been injured. It should be reported to the correct lead so the crew can check the ramp, case brakes, parking location, or work process before it happens again.

## **Scenario 4: The Safety Briefing**

During the safety briefing, the lead says hard hats are required near the stage until overhead work is complete. Later, you see another new stagehand walking into that area without a hard hat.

**Question:** What is the professional response?

**Best Answer:**  
 Do not ignore it or create conflict. Calmly warn the worker if appropriate and report the issue to the correct lead. The briefing information should be applied during the call, and PPE requirements should be followed.

## **Scenario 5: The Untrained Equipment Request**

Someone asks you to move an aerial lift “just a few feet.” You have never been trained or authorized to operate it.

**Question:** What should you say or do?

**Best Answer:**  
 Do not operate the lift. Say clearly, “I have not been trained or authorized to operate that equipment. We need someone qualified.” Then follow direction from the correct lead.
